- **Adding a stage declaration to the shipped workflow breaks the end-to-end tests of every check already declared on that stage, and the repair is to mirror the workflow, not to weaken the test.** `tests/test_stage_output_ownership.py`'s two ownership tests drive real `run_story` calls whose subject is that the ownership rule reads `created` alone; against the shipped workflow they now also pick up the new `revert_check` key, and their synthetic records name paths with no version at HEAD, so the revert check correctly escalates with *could not run* and the runs no longer complete. story-017 repaired them with an `ownership_only` helper built on that file's existing `mirror_harness`, which runs each against a copy of the workflow with the implementer's `revert_check` popped; every assertion and every record is unchanged and the docstring says why. That is the pattern to reuse: a test whose subject is one check is run against a workflow declaring only that check, so the next declaration does not silently retarget it. The general form is the story-013 ripple bullet above — a per-story test that goes red on landing gets repointed, never relaxed — and demonstrating the repair was *forced* is the same revert the check itself performs.
- **A fact `state.json` does not carry is added to `state.json`; it does not get a shadow file.** story-020 needed three facts a resume cannot derive — the story artifact as the run first read it (`story_digest`), the commit the escalation made (`escalation_commit`), and the harness revision at the moment it escalated (`harness_revision`) — and put all three on `RunState` rather than beside it. Each is defaulted, so `RunState(**json)` still loads a state file written before this story, and **empty means "not established" at every reader** rather than being a value to compare against. That single convention is what makes the resume guard's bias correct by construction instead of by three separate checks. The story added no new artifact at all: the run directory already holds every stage artifact and the attempt archive, and no second record of where execution stands was created.
- `story_digest(story_text)` is taken from the same text `read_story` was handed, so the digest and the run's one reading describe one artifact — the same "exactly one mechanism reads a story artifact" rule, applied to hashing it. It is recorded when the run's state is first created, and it feeds a refusal *message*. It does not authorize a resume, does not trigger one, and no line in `run_story` routes on it.
- **The resume guard refuses only on positive evidence of sameness.** Every comparison in `unchanged_since_escalation` must be establishable before it can contribute, and a comparison that cannot be made returns the empty list rather than a partial verdict. The bias is deliberate and one-directional: under-refusing costs a wasted run the developer asked for, over-refusing blocks a resume the developer needs, and only the second is a failure the harness inflicts. A harness root that is not a git repository is the case to keep working — it produces no refusal. story-034 extended that bias rather than bending it: `same_repository` reports not-shared whenever either `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` fails, and the guard then falls through to the comparison it made before.
- **A guard that cannot fire in the configuration it is developed under is a guard nobody is testing.** `unchanged_since_escalation` was correct for a separate-repository deployment and structurally unable to refuse in this one, and the gap surfaced only as a resume nobody questioned. The repair was to recognise the shared-root case and let an existing comparison stand for the redundant one, not to add a fourth comparison or a second definition of "changed" — see "When the harness is its own target" above for the rejected tree hash and the rejected relocation of `state.harness_revision`. **When a check reads a fact the run itself writes, ask which deployment it was reasoned about in before trusting it in another.**
- **Both terminal commits now establish what they commit, and neither was changed to do it.** story-020 stated the limit — `git add -A` stages whatever the working tree holds — in the coordinator where the commits are made and in a test worded as a property of the coordinator rather than of one commit, and story-021 closed it from the other end: the clean-tree pre-flight establishes the tree *before* any stage runs, so staging everything and staging what the run produced are the same set. `_complete` and `_escalate` are byte-for-byte unchanged. The stated limit that survives is the pre-flight's one exclusion, a resumed crashed run, stated in the same place and driven by the same kind of test. **A stated limit a reader meets where the behaviour is, and a test that goes red when the statement stops being true, is the shape to reuse for any limit a story knowingly leaves open** — and the corollary story-020 predicted held: the assertion was repointed to the guarantee that now holds, keeping its subject, its strictness and its stray file, rather than deleted.
- **Deferring a decision to the story that owns it worked.** story-020 deliberately wrote no rule about which runs the future clean-tree pre-flight applies to, so that deciding it by accident could not happen; story-021 then decided it in one place, recorded above under "The tree a run starts from". Two requests planned together, with the shared question named in both and answered in neither until the second landed, is the shape that avoided it.
- A narrowness in the guard, recorded rather than fixed: `unchanged_since_escalation` runs before `_checkout_story_branch`, so its `HEAD~1` comparison reads whatever branch the developer is standing on rather than `state.branch`. A developer who escalates, checks out `main` to think about it — the act this story exists to make safe — and then re-runs the story gets a resume rather than the refusal. Whether that is a miss depends on reading "the target tree" as the branch (the technical plan says "the branch's HEAD", which would make it a one-line change to `f"{state.branch}~1"`) or as the working tree (which after the checkout genuinely has changed). It is left as it is because the story's stated bias is toward under-refusing, and it is written down here so the next reader decides it deliberately.
- Landing story-020 turned three existing assertions red and each was **repointed, not relaxed** — the standing ripple rule. `tests/test_revert_baseline.py` asserted `state.json`'s field set exactly; its subject was that the *baseline* added no field, so it now asserts the story-019 fields are a subset and that no field names the baseline. `tests/test_retry_history.py` compared two runs' whole state; it now compares field by field excluding `escalation_commit`, because two copies of one repository escalate to different shas for reasons unrelated to routing, and the exclusion is a named set so a future field is included by default. `tests/test_attempt_archiving.py` asserted `"attempts"` appears once in the coordinator and inside `archive_attempt`; the literal moved to `attempt_dir`, so it now asserts the literal is in the helper and that `archive_attempt` calls it — the guarantee is unchanged and now exact. `tests/test_coordinator_contract.py` is the deliberate edit rather than a ripple: the new fields and the fact that `escalated` is an *ending* status but no longer a final one are changes to the standing contract, which is what that file exists to make explicit.
- **An act the harness depends on is performed by the harness, not asked of an agent.** `prompts/planner.md` step 5 used to tell the planner to commit the story artifact it wrote. That is guidance to an agent — the mechanism this repository has watched fail repeatedly, and the same reasoning behind "a rule only a prompt states is a rule the harness cannot see broken" — and it did not push, so the commit sat local until something else carried it. story-023 moved the act into `scripts/l5-plan`, and the prompt now says outright that the harness commits and pushes. **One act, one mechanism**: the prompt must not reacquire a commit instruction while the script commits, in either direction.
- **The same move, made again at the seam between planning and running.** `prompts/planner.md` used to close by telling the planner to print `scripts/l5-run <story-id>`, and the developer read the id off the screen and typed the command back. The script that had just committed the artifact was the thing best placed to offer to run it, so since story-059 it does, and the prompt's closing sentence names only the story id. The command it used to ask the agent for is now printed by the script or acted on directly, and the same one-act-one-mechanism rule applies in both directions: the prompt must not reacquire the command while the script prints it.
- **`l5-plan` commits from what appeared on disk, never from the session's exit status.** The stories directory is snapshotted before the session and the files that appeared under it afterwards are the artifacts. A session that wrote a plan and exited non-zero still commits it — committing is done before the status is consulted at all, and the statuses are combined afterwards so the session's own status wins. (The first implementation wrote `sys.exit(status or report(...))`, which short-circuits and skipped the commit on a non-zero session; the ordering is the point, not a detail.) A session that added nothing commits nothing, pushes nothing, and says so — that is a correct outcome, not a failure. A session that only *edited* an existing artifact is the same case, because the script commits only files it caused to appear. **What story-023 did not do, story-025 did**: appearance is still the whole test of *which* artifacts are the session's, but since story-025 an artifact that does not read back cleanly is no longer committed. The story-023 rule that the parse "feeds the commit subject only and gates nothing" is now true of `plan_commit` alone — `commit_subject` still falls back to `Plan story-NNN` on a parse failure, and that guarantee is still asserted directly on it — while the gate lives one step earlier in `plan_validation`, so a failing artifact never reaches the commit at all. `.harness/requests/11-plan-time-validation.md` and story-018 are the history behind it; story-025 is where it landed, and it landed in the script rather than as a separate command.
- **A failed push is loud and changes nothing.** No remote configured and a rejected push are both reported naming the remote and the branch, both leave the commit intact and reachable, and neither rolls back, amends or resets. Falling back to a local-only commit is the situation the repository already lived with, so it is no worse than the status quo; refusing or unwinding would be worse. The script exits non-zero when the commit succeeded and the push did not, so it is noticed without anything being undone. There is deliberately **no flag, environment variable or config key** to skip either the commit or the push — an act that is a mechanism has no opt-out, or it is guidance again.
- **Pushing is what removes an observed divergence, and it is why the push is not optional.** The planner committed on `main` locally, the story branch was cut from that commit, the PR rebased it to a new sha, and `git pull --ff-only` afterwards refused with local `main` ahead 1, behind 2 — seen on story-017, story-020 and story-022, each needing a diagnosis and a `git reset --hard origin/main` to clear. With the artifact on `origin/main` before the run starts, the branch is cut from a commit already upstream and no duplicate is created.
- **This is the half of story-021's assumption that now holds deterministically.** story-021 declines to exempt the story artifact's path from its clean-tree pre-flight, reasoning that `l5-plan` already commits the artifact, and its acceptance criterion then makes an uncommitted artifact refuse the run. Until story-023 that made a deterministic refusal conditional on a probabilistic commit: when the planner forgot, the developer met a refusal and committed by hand. story-023 changes nothing story-021 defines — not the clean-tree check, not its exemptions — it makes the assumption behind it true.
- **A check belongs at the moment a human is still present to act on it.** The two structural checks story-025 moves to plan time already existed at pre-flight; nothing about *what* they refuse changed. What changed is that they now run while the interactive session's developer is still there, instead of after the one exchange that could have repaired the artifact had closed. This is the reason the same check may legitimately run twice and the reason plan time may hold a check pre-flight does not: `strictness_problems` is a plan-time-only class precisely because adding it to pre-flight would start refusing committed artifacts that already ran. When adding a check, ask where the cheapest repair is available, not only where the run would notice.
- **When two entry points refuse the same defect, they must refuse it through the same function, not with the same words.** `refuse` and `refuse_bad_story` were promoted out of the coordinator's private namespace so `l5-plan` prints what `l5-run`'s pre-flight prints, for the same artifact and the same defect, from one code path. Two messages that agree today are two messages that can drift — the identical failure mode as a prompt restating an enforced contract, one layer down. The test for it compares the two captured outputs rather than comparing either against a literal, so a reworded message stays green and a *second* message goes red.
- **A test that records behaviour a later story deliberately reverses is split, not deleted.** story-023's `test_an_unparseable_artifact_is_still_committed_with_the_fallback_subject` asserted exactly what story-025 reverses. Neither half was lost: the fallback-subject guarantee is now asserted directly on `plan_commit.commit_subject`, where it lives and where it is still true, and a new-named sibling asserts the new end-to-end behaviour through the real script — exit non-zero, `HEAD` unmoved, the artifact still on disk. Reverting either change re-breaks the suite. The other forced repair went the other way and is worth the contrast: `test_the_planner_prompt_no_longer_asks_the_planner_to_commit` asserts the literal `"l5-plan commits"` appears in `planner.md`, so the *prompt* was reworded to "when it is valid, l5-plan commits it" and the test is byte-for-byte unchanged with its control still passing. Repair the artifact when the test's subject still holds; split the test when its subject genuinely changed.
- **A pre-flight that constrains the tree a run starts from lands as a wide test ripple, and the ripple is setup, not assertions.** Landing story-021's check turned 69 existing tests across ten files red, and almost all of them for one reason: the shared `target_root` fixture commits at init, but individual tests then installed a story artifact or overrode a config key and ran without committing, so the run started from a tree it could not account for. That setup *is* part of the repository the run starts from, so the fix is to commit it — one `commit_setup(root, message)` in `tests/conftest.py`, beside the shared baseline resolution, called from each file's existing setup helper rather than at every call site. Not one assertion was weakened, skipped or deleted. Expect this shape from any future check on pre-run state: budget for the fixtures, not for the subjects.
- **A test whose subject is a guard must not be routed through the setup that satisfies a different guard.** story-020's resume tests split cleanly here: the sixteen whose subject is what the resume *does* go through `ready_to_resume`, which commits the setup so the resume starts clean, while the guard-level tests call `unchanged_since_escalation` directly, never meet the pre-flight, and keep `change_the_code` meaning what it always meant there — a dirty tree the guard reads as changed. In one case the commit strengthened the test rather than accommodating it: without it, `test_a_resume_whose_archive_directory_exists_refuses_naming_it` would have met the clean-tree refusal and its message assertion would have been checking the wrong refusal entirely. When a new pre-flight makes a test green by refusing earlier, check which refusal the assertions are reading.
- **Two more `HEAD`-baseline traps surfaced under story-021, in files that had already been repaired for other reasons.** Both resolved their "after" side from today's working tree, so editing a file they bound made them red for a change their own story has nothing to say about: `tests/test_schema_inventory_location.py`'s inventory-bound assertion now reads `_endpoint_text(rel)`, the helper already beside it and already used by its two neighbours, and `tests/test_contract_assertions_bite.py` gained `story_011_at_this_storys_endpoint()`, the counterpart of its existing `story_011_before_this_story()`, resolved through the shared `conftest.story_commit_range` and falling back to the working tree while the story is in flight. Expected sets unchanged in both. This is the same bullet as the story-012 ripple wearing a third hat — an assertion about what a story did must be bounded at *both* ends of that story's commit range, and the repairs have to be repeated per file because the trap is written into each assertion rather than into a shared helper.
- story-021's own run was not governed by the check it adds, for the fifth time in this pattern's history (`may_not_create`, `clean_clone`, `revert_check`, the pre-stage baseline, and now the clean-tree pre-flight). The cause is one step earlier here than in the other four: they were stale *workflow declarations*, while this is the running coordinator process having imported `story_coordinator` before the check existed in it, the same staleness the `context_assembler` bullet describes. Enforcement begins with the next run. Expected, stated in the story's constraints, and not a defect.
- A narrowness recorded rather than fixed: `dirty_paths` strips the surrounding quotes from a quoted porcelain path but does not un-escape the C-style escapes git writes inside one, so a filename containing a literal backslash or newline is named slightly wrong in the refusal message. It never affects *whether* a run is refused — the path is still listed, and the list being non-empty is the whole decision — so it is message text only. Close it in the story that has a reason to.
- A second narrowness recorded rather than fixed: `plan_validation.artifact_problems` reads each artifact with `Path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")`, so an artifact that is not valid UTF-8 raises rather than being reported as a problem. Nothing is committed on that path either, so no story-025 criterion is violated, but the behaviour is exercised by no test and asserted nowhere. Close it in the story that has a reason to, and prefer reporting it as a problem over letting it raise.
- **Terminal resize behaviour under the new process model is confirmed by hand, not asserted by a test.** A test here could not observe SIGWINCH reaching the inherited session or the redraw that follows, and an assertion that cannot observe its subject is the failure mode `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py` exists about. story-023 states this as unverified rather than claiming it; the same is true of the clean-clone suite run for that story, which is the coordinator's own end-of-run check and cannot execute before the story commits.
- **The `HEAD`-baseline trap surfaced twice more under story-024, and the count is now the argument.** `tests/test_escalation_resume.py` and `tests/test_foreign_work_refusal.py` each assert "my story did not change `_escalate`" by comparing a pre-story module against **today's working tree** — so story-024 changing `_escalate`, which those stories have nothing to say about, made both red. Each gained an `at_story_endpoint(path)` counterpart to its existing `pre_story(path)`, resolved through the shared `conftest.story_commit_range` and falling back to the working tree while the story is in flight, and the two files' identical loader bodies were merged rather than duplicated a third time. Subjects, strictness and controls unchanged; the comparisons are now bounded at both ends of their own story's commit range. That is the same repair for the fourth time (story-012's ripple, story-021's two files, now these two), always after the fact and always per file. **The next story with a reason to touch this should move the both-ends resolution into `tests/conftest.py` beside `story_commit_range` and have the per-story files call it**, because the trap is currently written into each assertion rather than into a shared helper, and re-deriving it per file is what keeps producing the failure.
- **The second resolution of a story's own commit range is folded into the shared one, and what is *not* duplication is named so it is not folded next.** story-015 put that resolution in `tests/conftest.py`; `tests/test_execution_history.py` kept `story_revision()`, which answered the same question by a different mechanism — walking the coordinator's own history for the oldest revision whose source contains `"execution-history"`. The duplication was created by story-015 landing rather than by anything wrong in it: that file is not one of the four story-015 repairs, so it was pinned in `do_not_modify` correctly. Both resolved the same pair (`2239a23..973fdb5`), verified by running the deleted helper out of the pre-story source rather than by reading it, so the fold is a change of mechanism and not of subject. story-026 deletes `story_revision()` and points its one caller, `test_no_prompt_template_was_changed_by_this_story`, at `conftest.story_diff`; the assertion guards the same three paths (`prompts/`, `workflows/`, `rules/`) at the same strictness, and only where its two bounds come from moved. The shared mechanism is the better one because it needs no per-story marker string — a marker has to be chosen, and stays true only until someone edits the line it names. **`pre_story_coordinator_source` was deliberately not folded**: it resolves the pre-story coordinator's *source text*, which the differential module loading needs, while the shared helper resolves *commits*. Two different questions, not one fact in two places; `coordinator_source_at` and `pre_story_revision` stayed with it, unchanged. Read this bullet before folding anything else that looks like a duplicate baseline: the test is whether the two answer the same question, not whether they both call git. **story-029 reverses the decision, and the reasoning above is why it survived as long as it did.** "Text, not commits" is a real distinction and it correctly kept the *fold* from happening; what it did not license is eleven private copies of the text-reading question. story-029 deletes all three helpers along with the loading they existed to feed, and answers the text question once in `tests/conftest.py` — `repository_file_at(relative, revision= | validation_file=, bound=)`, with `function_source`/`function_source_at` on top. Two questions, two shared answers, both in the one file. Nothing here changes the commit-range fold; what changes is that "different question" stopped being a reason for a private helper.
- Landing the fold turned one assertion red and the repair was the standing one, for the fifth time. `tests/test_contract_assertions_bite.py`'s `test_the_functions_that_did_change_are_only_those_that_took_the_baseline` read its "after" side from today's working tree, so deleting a helper story-016 has nothing to say about made it red; its upper bound moved onto `story_011_at_this_storys_endpoint()`, the counterpart its three siblings already used. Subject, strictness, `REMOVED_TESTS` and `REMOVED_HELPERS` unchanged. The cost of the repair is worth knowing: both of that comparison's bounds are now frozen commits, so it can no longer be turned red by any future change in this repository, and its ability to fail is demonstrated only under monkeypatched bounds. That is a property it now shares with its already-accepted sibling, and it is the price of bounding an assertion at both ends of a finished story — the assertion is about a past commit range, so once that range is history there is no live history left to violate it. `story_revision` was **not** added to `REMOVED_HELPERS`: that list records what *story-016* removed, and story-016 did not remove it. The intact-list in the same file lost `"def story_revision"` and kept its other seven entries, with the reason recorded beside the list.
- **The both-ends move the story-024 bullet asks for is still outstanding.** story-026 folded a *duplicate resolution*, which is a different and smaller thing than moving the both-ends bounding idiom into `tests/conftest.py` beside `story_commit_range`. Every per-story file still writes its own `at_story_endpoint`/`pre_story` pair, and the repair above is the fifth instance of re-deriving it. The ask stood as written through story-027's sixth instance and is **answered by story-029**, which put `repository_file_at` beside `story_commit_range` and deleted all eleven private copies — see the bullet at the end of this file. The ask took four stories and seven re-derivations to be acted on, which is the argument for treating "record it and move on" as a debt with interest rather than a resolution.
- **An assertion that searches a whole document weakens as the document grows.** `tests/test_coordinator_contract.py` checked that `escalation-summary.md` points at `events.log` and the `verification/` directory by searching the entire summary. Once Recommended Investigation lists the run's artifacts and Outstanding Issues names `verification/iteration-N.json`, a whole-text search is satisfied by those mentions, and story-016's "stops pointing at the verification directory" mutation would have stayed green against a summary that had genuinely stopped pointing. Both checks now read inside the `## Where to Look` section, which is what that part of the contract always meant. When a contract asserts that a *section* says something, scope the search to that section on the way in — the vacuous version is indistinguishable from the strict one until something else in the file happens to say the same word.
- **The `HEAD`-baseline trap surfaced again under story-027, and the outstanding ask above is now overdue.** `tests/test_escalation_resume.py::test_the_completion_commit_is_byte_for_byte_the_code_it_was` asserts that *story-020* did not change `_complete`, as exact `inspect.getsource` equality against a pre-story-020 module — but read its "after" side from today's working tree, so extracting `_complete`'s commit message, which story-020 has nothing to say about, made it red. It was repointed to `endpoint_coordinator(tmp_path)`, the counterpart helper already defined in that file and already used by one of its siblings, resolved through the shared `conftest.story_commit_range`; subject, strictness and its `_escalate` negative control are unchanged, and reverting the repoint re-breaks the suite. That is the sixth instance of the same repair, in a file already repaired once for it. The both-ends bullet above asks for the idiom to move into `tests/conftest.py`; every instance since has been another per-file re-derivation, which is exactly what the ask predicts will keep happening.
- **What `_complete` commits is now something the coordinator is held to, in `tests/test_coordinator_contract.py`.** The completion commit's subject shape and marker sentence stopped being an implementation detail the moment a pre-flight recognizes them: a reworded commit message would silently stop the finished-branch check from ever refusing, with no test of the check itself going red. Per that file's standing purpose, the shape is stated directly there rather than as equality with a past implementation, so a later story that means to change it edits the contract deliberately.
- story-027's own run was not governed by the check it adds, for the sixth time in this pattern's history (`may_not_create`, `clean_clone`, `revert_check`, the pre-stage baseline, the clean-tree pre-flight, and now the finished-branch pre-flight). The cause is the story-021 one rather than the stale-workflow one: the coordinator process imports `story_coordinator` at start, so the running coordinator carried the pre-flight as it stood before this story. Enforcement begins with the next run. Expected, stated in the story's constraints, and not a defect.
- **A check that names helpers is evaded by renaming a helper; a check that names language constructs is not.** This is the sharpest fact story-029 leaves behind, and it is not a general preference for AST scans — the repository already had one. `tests/test_contract_assertions_bite.py` carried `test_no_module_under_tests_loads_a_coordinator_out_of_git_history`, matching on two name lists, `HISTORY_SOURCE_READERS` and `MODULE_LOADERS`. All four modules that later shipped the practice defined their own helpers under different names and passed it, in good faith and without anyone noticing, which is worse than the check not existing: a green name-matching scan reads as coverage. story-029 deletes it and replaces it with two structural scans in `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py`. `module_construction` names the closed set of constructs that are the only ways to build or run a module in-process — `spec_from_file_location`, `spec_from_loader`, `module_from_spec`, `exec_module`, `SourceFileLoader`, `ModuleType`, `import_module`, `run_path`/`run_module`, and bare `exec`/`eval`/`compile`/`__import__` (qualified `re.compile` and `ast.literal_eval` are not builtins, which is what keeps them out). `git_text_reads` names the subcommands that return a file's text at a revision, and reads both the spawned form and the *wrapped* form (`git(REPO_ROOT, "show", f"{rev}:{path}")`) by shape rather than by the wrapper's name — the wrapped form is what all four offending modules actually wrote. Neither scan can be evaded by renaming, because neither names a helper. **When choosing what a mechanical check matches on, ask what the smallest edit is that keeps the behaviour and clears the check**; if that edit is a rename, the check is not the one to write.
- **The two scans are two rules, not one rule with two halves.** `module_construction` (exempting `conftest.py` via `CONSTRUCTION_EXEMPT_MODULES`) exists so no test builds a module at runtime; `git_text_reads` (exempting `conftest.py` via `READER_EXEMPT_MODULES`) exists so the reader lives in one place. They share a file, a glob and an exemption *value*, and nothing else: separate constants, disjoint vocabularies, neither calling the other, and a test reads that separateness off the source. The reason to keep them apart is that they fail for different reasons and will be relaxed or widened at different times — a scan described as drawing its enforcement from another is a scan whose limits are the union of both and whose repair is neither's. Each carries its own planted violations that it reports, a renamed-helpers probe it still reports, benign probes it leaves alone, and — following the narrowness `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py` already states about itself — its own written limits: source run in a subprocess rather than in-process, historical text written to a file and passed in as a path, deliberate obfuscation of a banned construct, and the standing not-tamper-proof limit (an edit deleting a check alongside a genuinely forced repair satisfies the revert rule's own definition of forced, at any granularity — the same limit recorded for the revert check, and not closable here).
- **The evidence that a check would have caught what it is for is the check reporting the historical instances, recovered at the baseline.** Both scans are run over the four modules as they stood at story-029's baseline revision (`CARRIED_THE_PRACTICE`, resolved through the shared range applied to the story's own validation file) and asserted to report every one. Everything else a new scan can say about itself — planted violations, benign probes, a clean current suite — is about its reach in the abstract. This is the only assertion that says it would have caught the thing that actually happened. Do this for any check written in response to an observed miss; the regression-set bullet above is the same rule for the honesty scan, and it is now the standing shape.
- **The both-ends bounding idiom is finally shared, six re-derivations after the ask.** `repository_file_at(relative, *, revision=)` or `(relative, *, validation_file=, bound=)` in `tests/conftest.py` answers "this repository file's text at one end of a story's own commit range", with the in-flight fallback to the working tree in one place instead of eleven; `function_source(source, name)` and `function_source_at(...)` sit on top for a named function's text. Eleven private copies are gone. Two shared loaders landed with it and their signatures carry the story's intent: `load_mutant(source_path, replacements, ...)` takes **a working-tree path and its replacements**, not arbitrary source text, so recovered history is not a value it naturally accepts, and `load_script(script_name)` covers the extensionless-script case. `_load_module` in `conftest.py` is now the single module-construction site under `tests/`, and `runnable_against_current_modules` is deleted rather than widened or repaired. The order the story ran in is the reusable part and is the repository's own rule from the shelf-life bullet: every replacement assertion landed and was shown red on violation *before* the comparison it replaced was deleted, and the capability was deleted last. Twelve comparisons were restated, not the six the plan predicted — `tests/test_escalation_resume.py` carried three the plan did not enumerate — and six text-only assertions retargeted rather than four. **A plan that counts instances of a practice will undercount it; the count is a starting point, not the scope.**
- **A defect whose only account is a test has lost its account when the test is deleted.** Two of the deleted instruments were defect reproductions. Their subjects are recorded as frozen evidence under `.harness/runs-archive/story-021-artifact-absorbed/` and `.harness/runs-archive/story-027-rerun-onto-finished-branch/`, each a `README.md` plus a machine-readable `evidence.json` that an assertion reads, and story-021's is checked claim by claim against the real observed instance already committed under `.harness/runs-archive/story-013-vacuous-tests/`. They are records rather than re-runs for a reason worth keeping: story-029's implementer tried to capture live output from both recovered coordinators first and **neither executes** — each raises `TypeError` inside `clean_clone_check`, because `clean_clone` became an object-valued workflow declaration after their stories. That is the decay this whole sequence argues about, observed rather than predicted. Each record states in its own `why_it_cannot_be_rerun` field that it was not produced by re-running the code, and a test holds both records to saying it.
- **Two edits landed outside story-029's `scope.modify`, each forced by the story's own acceptance criteria.** `tests/test_shared_baseline_resolution.py`'s `pre_repair_source` was a private copy of the history reader, and `tests/test_stage_output_ownership.py`'s `era_constant` used `exec` on a line lifted out of another module — the construction scan cannot be written flat with `exec` excluded, since `ns={}; exec(recovered, ns)` is precisely the evasion. Each was repointed minimally (the second onto `ast.literal_eval`), subject, strictness and raise-on-missing behaviour unchanged, and running the shipped scans over each file's pre-edit text reports exactly one flag apiece — so reverting either puts a module back in violation. This is the ripple rule again, with one addition: **a story that adds a flat structural ban must expect the ban to reach files its plan did not scope**, because the plan enumerated the practice's *instances* and the ban covers its *shape*.
- **One acceptance criterion could not be satisfied literally, and the conflict is between two of the story's own instructions.** The criterion asks that grep for `runnable_against_current_modules`, `pre_story_coordinator_source`, `coordinator_source_at` and `pre_story_revision` over `tests/` return nothing. The first is gone entirely. The other three survive only as **string literals inside assertions bounded at finished commit ranges** — story-016's intact-list and story-026's survivor list — which the same story's task 16 says to *repoint keeping subject and strictness*, not to delete, and an assertion that those names were present at a past revision cannot be written without spelling them. No definition, call, plain-name reference, attribute or import of any of the four survives, and a test asserts that with a control. Recorded because the shape recurs: **a "grep returns nothing" criterion collides with any assertion whose subject is that the name used to exist**, and the criterion should be written as "no definition, call or import" when a historical assertion is also in scope.
- story-029's own run was not governed by the scans it adds, for the seventh time in this pattern's history, and for a reason particular to it rather than the stale-workflow or stale-import one: the suite it runs is the suite it is changing. Expected, stated in the story's constraints, and not a defect.
- **A standing "nothing in orchestration does X" assertion is repaired by exempting the one module that now does, held shut from both sides — not by deleting the assertion or splitting the test.** story-032 forced two repairs under `tests/`, both in `tests/test_stage_output_ownership.py` and both call-site repairs rather than new coverage. `test_the_ownership_check_names_no_stage` referenced `story_coordinator._granted_prefixes` by name and now references `granted_paths`, the same function renamed (`grant_covers` was tried in that tuple and removed: the test's anti-vacuity guard requires the stripped body to contain `created` or `stage`, and `grant_covers` contains neither by construction — it takes no stage at all, which is the property the test is about). `test_nothing_in_orchestration_reads_the_plan_attribution` asserted that no orchestration module reads `likely_file_changes` or `technical_plan`, which this story deliberately reverses for exactly one module; story-007's actual subject — the attribution is advisory and no *run* compares it to what a stage did — is unchanged, so `plan_validation.py` is exempted **by name**, and the exemption is held shut from both directions: the exempt module must exist and must actually contain both names, and `story_coordinator.py` is asserted to contain neither. A one-sided exemption is a hole; a two-sided one is a statement about where the reader lives. The repair was also folded into the existing test rather than added as a new function, because `tests/test_shared_baseline_resolution.py` pins that file's test-name set — the correct reading of this stage's boundary, and the shape to expect when repairing a file whose name set another module holds fixed.
- **A fourth rule joins the three in `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py`, and what it reports is a *pairing* rather than either half.** `mutation_controls(source, module)` returns the same `Flag` records the others do, and flags only a function in which a revision-bounded read flows into a write to a path *and* the same function also executes source. Each half alone is legitimate and stays unreported: a bounded read that is only compared is what establishing a story's diff looks like, and a mutation of working-tree source written into a copy and run under pytest is the shape story-028's repair merged. A revision bound is recognised by shape and not by helper name, in both forms — a call carrying `revision=`/`bound=`, and a spawned git invocation asking for a file's text at a revision — so a caller that reaches history without the shared reader is still seen; the mutation loader is likewise matched by its `name=`/`tmp_path=` interface rather than its name, per the rename bullet above. The flow is followed inside one function, directly and through a `.replace(...)` of the read value; the execution half is followed one step into a top-level helper, because a control almost never spawns inline.
- **The pairing degenerates unless the read is excluded from counting as the execution.** A spawned `git show` *is* a subprocess call, so the first implementation let every git-form bounded read supply its own second half: a bounded read written to a path and never run was flagged in the git form and not in the keyword form — the same benign shape reported differently depending on how the bound was spelled. `_executing_functions` now skips any call that is itself a revision-bounded read, so some call *other than the read* has to run the text, and the reasoning is stated where the exclusion lives. The general form: **when a check pairs two constructs, ask whether either construct can match both halves of the pair**; if it can, the check reports the presence of one thing while claiming to report a relationship. It is held by a case rather than by prose — the benign parametrization carries the git form and the keyword form of the written-but-never-executed shape side by side.
- **This rule names no exempt module, and the absence is an argument rather than an oversight.** The two story-029 rules exempt `conftest.py` because it is the one place the thing they forbid is the correct answer. A mutation of pinned source has no such place: `load_mutant` takes a working-tree path and its replacements precisely so pinned text is not a value it accepts, so there is nowhere under `tests/` where this would be right. A test reads every `*_EXEMPT_MODULES` constant off the module's source and asserts the set is exactly the three that came before — so a fourth cannot be added without that test going red — and the prose beside it says why — an exemption list that is empty for a reason and one that is empty by accident read identically in code. The one known true positive is held per the recovered-at-the-baseline bullet above: the pre-repair text of `tests/test_git_history_loading_retired.py`, resolved through `conftest.repository_file_at` with `validation_file=tests/test_retry_routing.py` and `bound=BASELINE` — a story bound, no SHA in the source — is reported, and the same file in the working tree today is reported by nothing, so the regression set shows it flagged before its repair and clean after. story-029's two meta-assertions extend from two rules to three: no scan calls another, the vocabularies stay pairwise disjoint, and each rule states what it does not cover, all read off the module's source.
- **A test module is named for what it checks.** Thirty-four modules under `tests/` were named `test_story_<digits>`; story-038 renamed them to thirty-two named for their subjects, merging story-008's and story-009's into `tests/test_planner_injection.py` and story-014's and story-033's into `tests/test_clean_clone_check.py`. The number is meaningful only while the story is in flight — once it merges the name says somebody once worked on a numbered thing, and a reader looking for the revert check, the resume guard or the retry routing can only grep. Two modules keep the prefix and are correct: `tests/test_story_parser.py` and `tests/test_story_coordinator.py` are named for the story parser and the story coordinator, which are subjects. **The rename was the easy half.** `tests/conftest.py` identified a story by the add-commit of its validation file, so a bare `git mv` would have repointed every renamed module's baseline at the *rename* commit — each differential assertion then comparing the rename against its parent, finding nothing, and passing. The resolution was settled first (see the `STORY_ORIGINS` bullet above) and the renames followed; the scan below and the renames landed in the same story, because a scan added ahead of them fails against the files it exists to prevent and a half-renamed suite carries two conventions with no way to tell which modules were converted.
- **The convention is held by three mechanisms, and the prompt is deliberately the weakest of them.** A **plan-time refusal** — `plan_validation.naming_problems`, called from `artifact_problems` — leaves an artifact naming a `tests/test_story_<digits>` file uncommitted in the working tree with the problem printed, so the name is caught where a human is present to fix it. A **standing scan** in `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py` — `story_numbered_modules(directory)`, a glob over the directory rather than a comparison against a listing, since a listing is a second copy of the answer that goes stale silently — reports any module whose name matches `^test_story_\d+`, with the directory a parameter so the same search the live suite is held to runs over a planted violation, and with `NAMED_FOR_A_STORY_SUBJECT` naming the two files the over-match control is about. It is a *filename search*, not one of the file's four AST rules: it returns names rather than `Flag` records, names no exempt module, and is outside the three-rules meta-assertions. And **one positive sentence** in `prompts/tester.md` says to name a validation module for the behaviour it validates — it replaces the example the pattern used to be learned from, now that every story-numbered name is gone. Read the ordering as the point: prose in an injected document is the layer that had already failed twice in this repository, and it is the one not relied on. The same shape as the vacuous-baseline sequence — the prompt guidance is the general reminder, the mechanical checks are what hold.
- **Two literals, two homes, held equal by a test.** `STORY_NUMBERED_MODULE = re.compile(r"^test_story_\d+")` is defined in both `orchestration/plan_validation.py` and `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py`, because the plan-time refusal ships with the harness and the scan belongs to the suite; neither may import the other. `tests/test_validation_module_naming.py` holds the two to the same verdict over a shared name set, which is what stops the plan-time refusal and the standing scan from drifting into two conventions. If a third reader appears, that is the point at which the pattern should get one home rather than a third copy.
- **A reference naming a path at a pinned historical revision keeps its historical spelling.** The rename sweep rewrote only references naming a file *as it exists now* — in `tests/`, `orchestration/`, `scripts/`, `prompts/` and this document. A reference that reads a path's text at a pinned revision was left spelled as it was there, because that is the name the object has at that revision and rewriting it breaks the read; `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py`'s regression set and `STORY_ORIGINS` itself are full of these. Nothing under `.harness/stories/` or `.harness/runs-archive/` was touched: both record what was true when they were written, and history describing the past accurately is not drift.
- **A suite count recorded in a story is a measurement of the tree it was taken from, and the tree moves.** story-038's acceptance criterion recorded 2002 tests "before the rename", and reconciling the finished suite against it took two attempts. The number was correct when taken — the plan commit `abce051` collects exactly 2002 — but `426fe1f` ("Grant the verifier a self-route budget of 1") landed between the plan and the work, moving one declaration out of `BUDGETLESS` into `BUDGETED` and shrinking a parametrization by 3, so the branch base collects 1999. Every item of the difference to the finished count is accounted for by module: +3 for the naming scan and its two controls, +109 for this story's own validation. **The reconciliation that settles it is per-module collection counts between the two trees, not a total**, since a total can absorb a lost module against an added one; here 32 of the 34 renamed modules collect an identical count and the two that differ are the two named. Five test *names* disappeared and all five are merge disambiguations rather than deletions — two collisions in the story-008/story-009 merge renamed, three in the story-014/story-033 merge suffixed `_by_story_033`. When a story records a count, record the revision it was measured at with it; when a later stage cannot reproduce it, compare per module before concluding anything was lost.
- **story-040 forced the standing-assertion repair pattern twice more, and the collision was unavoidable rather than incidental.** A module whose deliverable is to declare `tests/` and `pytest` cannot do it without spelling them, so `tests/test_stage_output_ownership.py::test_no_path_prefix_is_named_in_orchestration_code` and `tests/test_clean_clone_check.py::test_no_test_command_string_appears_in_orchestration_code` both went red on `orchestration/harness_source.py` the moment it existed. Each was repaired the way the bullet above prescribes — a by-name exemption held shut from both sides: the exempt module must exist *and* must actually contain the literal, or the exemption is stale and the test goes red; every other module is held to the original assertion unchanged; and neither exemption widens past the one literal it is about, so `harness_source.py` is still held to `may_not_create` in the first and to `-m pytest` and `unittest` in the second. Both repairs were folded into the existing test functions rather than added as new ones, because `tests/test_shared_baseline_resolution.py` pins `test_stage_output_ownership.py`'s test-name set exactly. No assertion was weakened, skipped or deleted and no test function was added or removed — the pre-story total of 2196 was unchanged by the repairs, and the finished suite is 2248 with story-040's own 52 cases. **The general shape worth carrying forward:** a story that adds a *declaration* of forbidden literals will collide with every standing scan that forbids them, and the collision is the declaration working rather than a defect in either.
- **Moving the last stage changed what `_complete` could rely on, and the finding was worth more than the reorder's own diff.** With the documenter last, `_complete`'s `git add -A && git commit` all but always found a dirty tree. With the verifier last, a run that enters at the verifier — a resume, for instance — changes no repository file, so `git commit` with nothing staged fails and the run completes leaving **no completion commit on the branch**. That is not cosmetic: `completion_commits` is how story-027's pre-flight recognises a finished story, so the branch would have been re-runnable. The commit now carries `--allow-empty`, for the reason both escalation commits already do; the message, the report and the ordering are untouched, and `tests/test_rerun_refusal.py::test_a_resume_of_an_escalated_run_is_unaffected` is what caught it and what holds it. **The general shape: a stage order is load-bearing for anything downstream that assumed what the last stage leaves behind.** Everything else the plan asked to be confirmed — `archivable_artifacts`, the attempt archive, the completion report's evidence list, the resume path — behaved correctly with no change. The `--allow-empty` commit kept the completion on the *branch* and story-065 found it does not keep it on the *trunk*, which is why `_complete` now amends in the one case where the empty commit is all it would have written; see "The completion commit survives any merge" above.
- **Relaxing a reader turns some existing control into evidence, and the control is what must move.** story-065 let `completion_commits` find the completion subject anywhere in a message rather than in `%s` alone. `tests/test_rerun_refusal.py::test_a_multi_line_body_does_not_split_one_commit_into_two` plants a body line reading `story-999: not a subject` and asserted that reading the same commit for `story-999` yields nothing — true under the old rule, and under the new rule that line *is* evidence for story-999. The control was repointed to a story the message names nowhere; its subject and its strictness (`== []`) are unchanged, a comment beside it says what moved and why, and reverting the repoint re-breaks the suite. **Look for this before widening any matcher**: a test that was passing *because* of the narrowness you are removing reads as a regression when it goes red, and the repair is to re-establish what the control was about rather than to soften either side.
- **story-045 modified a file in its own `do_not_modify` list, and the conflict was with a standing rule rather than with the story.** `prompts/documenter.md` gained `{{retry_guidance}}`, because story-028's rule — every stage a route can name must be able to say it is on a retry, asserted by `tests/test_retry_routing.py::test_every_stage_that_can_receive_a_retry_declares_the_placeholders`, which reads the destinations off the workflow — became true of the documenter the moment it became a destination. The diff is one placeholder with a two-word label, mirroring what `tester.md` got in story-028 for exactly this reason; nothing about what the documenter writes, nor the content or schema of `documentation-report.md`, changed, and reverting the edit re-breaks the suite. It was recorded as a stated deviation for the verifier to adjudicate rather than made silently, in the shape story-012's took, and the verifier accepted it. **A story that makes a stage a retry destination must expect that stage's prompt to be in scope**, whatever its `do_not_modify` list says.
- **Three assertions whose subject was "the documenter never ran" were restated rather than deleted.** A run that fails the clean-clone check, a refused clean-clone check, and an escalation at the retry ceiling all used the documenter's absence as the observable; the reorder makes the documenter genuinely run in all three, so each now asserts the guarantee that actually survives — the run did not *complete*: no completion report, status escalated. Repointing rather than deleting is the standing pattern; what is new is the reminder that **an assertion phrased against a stage's position expires when the position moves**, so prefer the outcome the check is about. 23 existing modules were repaired for the reorder in total, with no assertion weakened, skipped or deleted, and where a list could be derived off the loaded workflow instead of restated it now is (`stages_from` in `tests/test_escalation_resume.py`, `THROUGH_DOCUMENTER` in `tests/test_changed_files_records.py`), so the next reorder does not need them edited again.
- story-045's own run was not governed by the reorder it lands, for the same stale-workflow reason story-007, story-028 and story-030 record: the coordinator loads the workflow at run start, so its documenter ran last and unjudged and its verifier could not evaluate this document. Expected, stated in the story's own constraints, and not a defect.
- **A sixth rule joins the five in `tests/test_baseline_honesty.py`, and it is about where an assertion's *inputs* come from.** The five before it are about a comparison that cannot fail, an instrument built out of history, where a file's historical text is read, what a control may mutate, and what a module may be called. `live_artifact_reads(source, module)` reports a path in one module that resolves a **live harness artifact** — the shipped workflow, `rules/execution-rules.json`, this repository's own `.harness/config.yaml`, a prompt template or a schema — by joining onto one of the module-level names that stand for this repository. It reuses `_names_the_repository_root`, the same recognition the first and third rules use, rather than a second copy of it, so a path a test built for itself under `tmp_path` or a local it assembled names none of those roots and is not this rule's business. It matches on the **shape of the path** and never on a helper name, in both join idioms the suite writes — the `/` operator and `.joinpath(...)`, the second of which story-004 forces in places — and only the outermost join of a chain is reported, so `REPO_ROOT / "prompts" / "tester.md"` is one read rather than two. **The observed case is story-047's own first half:** granting one stage a `max_self_routes` budget, a correct one-line change, reddened four assertions in a module with nothing to say about whether the grant was right. Adding a stage or renaming an artifact does the same to a different set.
- **This rule reports rather than forbids, and the grandfathered list is the whole difference.** Twenty modules read a live artifact this way when it landed, and story-047 converts **none** of them — the two stories it precedes do that. `GRANDFATHERED_LIVE_ARTIFACT_READERS` is asserted **equal** to what the scan reports over `tests/`, in both directions and as two explicit differences so the failure says which happened: a module that begins reading a live artifact is absent from the list and fails, and a module converted to a fixture stops being reported so its stale entry fails. A subset assertion either way lets one of those pass silently, which is the whole reason the contract is equality. **The list can therefore only shrink**, and that is its purpose — it is a burn-down signal in the shape `TEMPORARY_TIES` established, not a suppression list. It is also written out rather than derived, and a test reads that off the module's own AST: a list computed from the scan would equal it by construction and the equality would assert nothing.
- **The sixth rule's stated limits are load-bearing, because the grandfathered list is only honest if a reader knows what the scan cannot see.** Three, stated in the module where a reader meets them and each demonstrated by a control rather than only claimed. It cannot see **an equivalent read reached through a helper in another module** — `conftest.shipped_workflow(REPO_ROOT, ...)` and `harness_config.load_rules(REPO_ROOT)` resolve the same artifacts and are not reported, because the join happens in a module this scan is not looking at while it reads this one. It cannot tell **a subject from an input**, which is the central limit: an assertion *about* the shipped workflow must read it, and the scan says exactly the same thing about that read as about one that merely needed *a* workflow — asserted by handing it the identical expression twice and comparing the two reasons. And it does not **evaluate expressions or track values**, so a path built from a fixture parameter, an `os.environ` lookup or a rebound local is invisible. Every report is a place to ask the question, not a verdict; the standing not-tamper-proof limit applies here as everywhere else. Each of the five artifact families carries its own planted violation, because a scan that had quietly lost one would still pass a control over the others.
- **The prompt guidance and the scan landed in one story, deliberately, and the reason is this repository's own measurement.** `prompts/tester.md` gained the rule as a **question the tester can answer at the moment it writes an assertion** — *is the shipped artifact the subject of this assertion, or an input to it?* — with the instruction to build a fixture when it is an input, and a pointer at the three idioms the suite already provides: a mirrored harness root, a target built under a temporary directory, and a probe workflow derived from the shipped one by mutating the single declaration the test is about. It names existing practice rather than describing a principle. Guidance alone would not have held and the story did not pretend otherwise: the `git diff HEAD` rule was documented *and* injected into every stage prompt and shipped five more times, and the git-history-loader rule was documented and shipped three more times; both stopped when a scan landed. Same ordering as the vacuous-baseline and module-naming sequences — the prose is the general reminder, the scan is what holds. **Nothing asserts that a tester followed the guidance**, and the story says so rather than constructing a test that appears to; there is no deterministic way to check it.
- **The fixture rule does not reverse the derive-from-the-workflow rule, and the prompt has to say so or it reads as a reversal.** The standing rule is that a test writes no stage name, no restricted prefix and no artifact name of its own, deriving each from the workflow. A fixture satisfies it identically, because the fixture defines those names **once** and the test derives them from the fixture exactly as it would have from the shipped definition. What changes is *which* workflow the names come from, not whether they are derived — so a hard-coded `"implementer"` or `"changed-files.json"` is wrong either way, and the two rules cannot be read as contradicting each other. **One correction to the story's own premise, recorded because a later reader will meet the same gap:** its constraint said the derive-from-the-workflow guidance in `prompts/tester.md` stays and is reconciled with; that guidance was not in the template. The rule lived in practice and in this document. Nothing was written over — the reconciling paragraph states the rule and then reconciles it, which adds it to the prompt for the first time.
- **Why the tester's budget is two where every other budget in this workflow is one, and why the documenter was the stage left budget-less until story-060.** The tester is the stage most often asked to produce something it has not produced before — a new rule, a new idiom, a new fixture shape — and a stage learning what to produce is the one most likely to fail mechanically on a first attempt and then again while correcting itself: one unit covers the dropped connection, the second covers the first correction landing incomplete. **The grant was checked against story-036's compatibility guards before the story was written rather than assumed.** Granting the tester leaves the documenter as the budget-less stage, and the documenter sits inside `RERUN_STAGES`, so every mechanical failure class stays reachable at a stage declaring no budget and the property that such a stage escalates on the first failure with no self-route artifact, no try-suffixed prompt and no self-routed event still holds. That check is the one most easily lost when a budget is granted; make it explicitly before granting the next one. **story-060 granted the documenter its budget and the check no longer has that shape**, because story-048 had since moved the compatibility property onto a workflow `tests/test_self_routing_retry.py` builds for itself: the question to ask before the next grant is whether the property a stage's absence of a budget was demonstrating is held against a *built* definition, not whether the deployment still keeps a stage back to demonstrate it.
- **Why the documenter's budget is `1` and why it carries no recorded reason.** story-060 granted it as a precaution rather than a fix — no documenter mechanical failure has been observed, and the grant carries no judgement about the work. What argued for it is where the documenter sits since story-045 reordered the stages: it runs before verification, the verifier reads `documentation-report.md`, and the documentation retry category routes back to it, so a dropped connection there discards a run whose implementer and tester work is already green, which is this workflow's most expensive escalation to recover by hand. `1` is the common budget in this deployment, and the sibling-reason convention applies to a budget that *differs* from the common one, so the declaration records no `max_self_routes_reason` and the tester's `2` remains the only one a reader meets an explanation beside. `test_the_budgets_this_deployment_grants_are_recorded_where_a_reader_meets_them` and `test_a_recorded_reason_states_the_number_it_is_explaining` needed no edit for the same reason — both take the common budget as the `min` of the declared budgets and read the outliers, and the documenter's `1` is not one.
- **The grant emptied a deployment assertion's population, and the assertion was rewritten rather than deleted.** `tests/test_shipped_workflow_is_valid.py` asserted that this deployment leaves at least one stage unbudgeted; granting the last one made that unsatisfiable. It now states the decision that replaced it — every shipped stage declares a budget, and every declared budget is an integer count — under a name saying what it checks, with a docstring keeping its provenance (it arrived from `tests/test_self_routing_retry.py` when story-048 split configuration from mechanism) and naming where the displaced property now lives. **The general shape:** when a deployment change empties the population an assertion reads, the assertion is rewritten to state what replaced the fact, and the check it runs is extracted far enough that a control built from `tests/conftest.py`'s builder can put the same code to a definition that violates it — otherwise "every stage is budgeted" passes just as happily against a check that has stopped looking. `bool` is excluded from the count check on purpose: `isinstance(True, int)` holds, so a bare integer test would accept a declaration of `true` as a budget.
- **The literal-invocation-list ripple recurred exactly as story-047 predicted it would.** `tests/test_changed_files_records.py::test_documenter_without_a_record_escalates_as_a_missing_artifact` pinned a literal call list and a literal escalation reason, both of which assumed the documenter never runs again in place; the repair is the one the tester's sibling took — resolve the invocation count off the stage's own declaration through the `_invocations_before_escalating` helper that module already grew, and compose the expected reason from the declared budget so it stays an equality rather than being loosened to a substring check. The sibling `test_documenter_naming_a_blocked_path_escalates` needed nothing, because a blocked-path violation is not a mechanical failure and still escalates on the first invocation. **That the two now resolve their call lists differently is the mechanism working**, not drift: the difference between them is precisely what a self-route budget bounds.
- **Two latent defects the grant exposed, repaired rather than accommodated, and both were predicted in the story rather than discovered by the run.** `tests/test_changed_files_records.py::test_tester_without_record_escalates_before_verifier` pinned `runner.calls == ["implementer", "tester"]`, a literal list that assumed the tester never runs again in place; it now derives the invocation count from the stage's own declaration — first invocation plus declared budget — and adds the assertion that actually carries its name, that the verifier is not in the calls however many tries it took. `tests/test_self_routing_retry.py::test_a_stage_that_self_routed_does_not_spend_another_stages_budget` compared filename-sorted self-route evidence against workflow order, which agreed only while the second budgeted stage happened to sort after the first; it now compares a sorted multiset, since which stage name sorts first says nothing about whose budget was spent. **Both keep their subjects and their strictness — the repair is to how each resolves what it expects, not to what it claims** — which is the standing ripple rule, and the general shape is worth carrying: *an assertion that pins a literal sequence of stage invocations is an assertion about the workflow's budgets, whether or not it knows it.*
- story-047's own run was not governed by the budget it grants, for the reason the stale-workflow bullets record: the coordinator loaded the workflow at run start, so its own tester ran under no budget. The scan is a different case and needed no such note — it is part of the suite the story changes, so it governs from the moment it lands.
- **story-050's ripples were declared rather than absorbed, and the two that touched a contract are the ones to notice.** `tests/test_coordinator_contract.py` and `tests/test_escalation_resume.py` both assert the `RunState` field set *exactly*, so `guidance_in_force` had to be declared in each — which is what those files exist for, and the assertions stayed exact equality. `tests/test_self_routing_retry.py`'s enum assertion was exact set equality against the three mechanical failure classes it parametrizes; the fourth value has no mechanical plan there, so a `NON_MECHANICAL_FAILURES` constant names it with the reason it is excluded, and the assertion is now equality against both lists plus a disjointness check — still exact in both directions, still every mechanical class parametrized, and a fifth value belonging to neither list still fails. Its test was renamed because the old name asserted "three". **The general shape, beside story-047's:** *an assertion that names a count is an assertion about a set that can grow*, and the repair is to make it derive the set, not to loosen the comparison.
- **A docstring is in the AST, and a scan that reads source text will read it.** `tests/test_retry_history.py` reports any routing function whose unparsed text mentions `retry-history.json`, and `guidance_entries`'s first docstring said "the retry-history entry". It was reworded to "the record of the retry just taken" rather than exempted — the scan is right that no such function should name the artifact, and the wording it forced is the more accurate one anyway.
- **A new event on a passing run breaks every exact event-stream equality in the suite, and the repair is an insertion rather than a loosening.** `tests/test_foreign_work_refusal.py::CLEAN_RUN_EVENTS` and `tests/test_rerun_refusal.py::FIRST_RUN_EVENTS` each state the whole stream of a clean run, and both gained the `claim-support-checked` line the run now genuinely emits. Both stay exact equality over the whole stream in the same order, with nothing dropped and no membership or subset comparison substituted, and both modules' controls that these expectations can fail are untouched — so the repair is the same shape story-040's standing-assertion repairs took. The subject of each module is a refusal, and a run reaching the documenter under the shipped workflow now recording what the claim-support check saw is a fact about this deployment's workflow rather than about either subject.
- **A coordinator-computed fact does not have to act, and the claim-support check is where that becomes explicit.** The computed checks before it all end in an outcome the coordinator chooses: the clean-clone check reroutes a passing verdict, the revert check escalates, and story-050's defective-guidance check self-routes the verifier. This one writes its record, injects it and stops. The reason is what the fact is: the coordinator can be sure nothing tracked could support the claim, and cannot be sure whether the claim should have been made — so the decision stays with the stage that reads prose for a living. A check that half-read the prose in order to route would have been the alternative, and the design refuses it on story-050's terms: a check that half-works is worse than none.
- **A rebased-away sha still resolves in the working repository and is unreachable in a clone, so a pinned sha fails the clean-clone check and nothing before it.** The baseline bullets above already say a search survives a rebase or a squash where a pinned SHA does not, and that framing understates the hazard: it reads as decay that arrives later, when the actual failure is available immediately and only to a clone. story-051's first attempt read story-049's "converted 8 of 22 modules" sentence out of history at `1323aed~1`. That commit was rebased away — today `git branch -a --contains 1323aed` names no branch while the object still resolves here, because the reflog keeps it reachable in *this* repository and a clone carries only what a ref reaches. The suite passed locally and the clean clone failed with `fatal: invalid object name '1323aed~1'`, on the one test the story exists for and for a reason with nothing to do with the check it adds. The repair was to resolve the revision by **searching for the text**, not by repointing the sha at the surviving commit's parent — which would have been correct that day and fragile on the same terms the next. `revision_carrying(relative, *needles, repo=...)` in `tests/conftest.py` is that search: the newest revision at which **one line** carries every needle, walking `git log --format=%H -- <path>` newest-first through `repository_file_at`, raising `NothingToCompareAgainst` when nothing carries them so a failed search cannot degrade into reading the working tree, and skipping a revision where the path has no blob per the standing `git log -- <path>` guard. It lives in `conftest.py` because that is the module exempt from the git-text-read rule; a private copy would have been a second reader of history, which is the duplication the fold bullets exist against. **The general shape: the clean clone is the only thing that distinguishes an object your repository can reach from one the repository can, and a pinned sha is the one construct where those differ silently.**
- **The search that replaced the sha failed in the clean clone too, and the second door is worth more than the first: a document that describes a check quotes the check's own subject.** The repair above searched the document's history for a single phrase, newest revision first. In the working tree the newest revision carrying it is the one before the correction — the sentence the story exists for — because this story's documentation is still uncommitted. In the clean clone the story *is* committed, and the newest revision of this document is the story's own: the section written above quotes the figure the check is about while writing about story-049 and story-051, so the search answered with it and the test read a sentence it is not about. Per the implementer's summary the assertion that caught it was `assert 'story-048' in ['story-049', 'story-051']` — the two stories that section names. `revision_carrying` now takes several needles and requires them **on one line**, which is what makes the *sentence* rather than the phrase the thing found; a single-needle call behaves as it did, so the widening admits nothing the old form refused. **The general shape: a test that searches history for text is searching a corpus its own story is about to join, and an architecture document is the one file guaranteed to acquire the words of every check the harness adds.** The narrowing constraint — a needle set no later sentence carries together — is the one to reach for, not a sha.
- **story-055's ripples were declared rather than absorbed, and one of them is a scope deviation the story recorded rather than glossed.** `schemas/execution-history.schema.json` is not in that story's `scope.modify`, and it gained the two `event` enum values anyway, because a run's history is validated against it and an event kind it does not declare fails. Reusing the existing `note` kind is the repair story-051 records as wrong — sharing it made another module's control report this check instead of its own subject — and an event that routes execution is not honestly a note. The general shape, beside story-047's and story-050's: *a mechanism that appends a new kind of event has a schema dependency whether or not its scope names one.*
- **The other three ripples touched contracts, and each stayed exact.** `tests/test_coordinator_contract.py` and `tests/test_escalation_resume.py` both pin the `RunState` field set exactly — which is what those files exist for — so `correction_pass_count` is declared in each with its reason and both comparisons stay exact equality, the same repair `guidance_in_force` took in story-050. `tests/test_plan_commit.py::test_the_argument_list_handed_to_claude_is_what_the_exec_passed` compares today's `l5-plan` argv against the pre-story-023 script's, and the old script resolves no prose partial, so it renders `{{prose_layer}}` as the literal `None` where today's renders the partial's text. The repair subtracts exactly that one substitution and then asserts equality on every other argument and every other byte of the prompt, plus a new assertion that the two argv lists really do differ — **narrowed to what this story changed rather than loosened**, which is the standing ripple rule.
- **`tests/test_harness_layer_extraction.py` is now about which prompts carry *a* shared partial rather than about story-003's extraction**, and its docstring says so. The two partials' reach is asserted off prompts something actually rendered — a real run's rendered stage prompts, and the prompt `scripts/l5-plan` hands a session through a stub `claude` on `PATH` — rather than off the templates, **because a placeholder in a template that nothing resolves is not reach**. That is why the module joins `DECLARED_LIVE_ARTIFACT_READERS` with the shipped prompts as its subject: what it asserts is what this deployment ships, and the planner half drives the real script, which resolves this repository as its own harness root.
- **Why the prose rule is a second partial rather than a paragraph in the first.** The harness-layer partial addresses stages that mutate a tree; the prose rule addresses anything that writes prose a human later reads, which includes the planner — the one template no coordinator renders, and the stage that wrote the first of the stale counts the rule exists against. Folding it into `harness-layer.md` would have forced that partial's reach to widen to a non-stage prompt, which is a change to a distribution three stories rely on being narrow. Two partials with two audiences, resolved through one helper, keeps both reaches decidable by reading one file each. **The constraint that made it cheap: the prose partial carries no placeholder of its own**, so the narrower planner context renders it identically to a stage's.
- Verification rules never change between retries; retries narrow scope, they do not restart the workflow.
- Capacity exhaustion (rate limits) is a reason to wait, not to fail; budget ceilings are a reason to stop. Story-063 gave the second half of that a mechanism, and it obeys it literally: a budget stop escalates and nothing on that path retries, sleeps or backs off. See "A run has a cost ceiling".
- **Cost is a peer of the retry ceiling, not a reading of it.** `max_retries` and `max_self_routes` are untouched by story-063 and no ceiling is derived from either. The three bound different quantities — attempts, in-place re-runs, and money — and a run can hit any one of them with the other two unspent.
- **The ceilings are declared in the workflow definition, which is one place.** No ceiling value is read from the execution rules, from `.harness/config.yaml`, or from the target's configuration, and none is written in orchestration code; they come off the loaded workflow exactly as `may_not_create`, `clean_clone` and `max_self_routes` do.
